Rebuilding After Wildfires Means Rethinking Residential Design

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The SCI-Arc Resilient Futures Task Force was created to rethink standard rebuilding methods after the January 2025 fires in Los Angeles exposed deep weaknesses in Southern California's default residential standards. Bringing together teachers, researchers, and students, the initiative gives displaced homeowners direct, practical help with building safety, soil toxicity, emergency funding, and insurance claims through public community meetings.

The SCI-Arc Resilient Futures Task Force was created to rethink standard rebuilding methods after the January 2025 fires in Los Angeles exposed deep weaknesses in Southern California's default residential standards. Bringing together teachers, researchers, and students, the initiative gives displaced homeowners direct, practical help with building safety, soil toxicity, emergency funding, and insurance claims through public community meetings.

Nearly two years later, residents are still rebuilding their lives as wildfires become more frequent and severe. Instead of repeating past mistakes caused by outdated safety codes, standard construction habits, and insurance gaps, this crisis presents a critical moment to create safer, long-term architectural designs in fire-prone areas.

Nearly two years later, residents are still rebuilding their lives as wildfires become more frequent and severe. Instead of repeating past mistakes caused by outdated safety codes, standard construction habits, and insurance gaps, this crisis presents a critical moment to create safer, long-term architectural designs in fire-prone areas.

SCI-Arc Harnesses Global Expertise for Resilient Building

Grounded in the institution's tradition of critical design research, the Resilient Futures Task Force connects academic expertise directly with real-world disaster recovery. The coalition brings together an international network of architects, structural engineers, scientists, material specialists, non-profit developers, and financial strategists. Key collaborators and participating institutions include Code Red Consultants, Angel City Lumber, SGH, JPMorgan Chase, Century Housing, Clearinghouse CDFI, Builders FirstSource, Plant Prefab, Sexsmith Architects, Timberlab, ReSAWN, Urban Machine, and TU Delft.

Grounded in the institution's tradition of critical design research, the Resilient Futures Task Force connects academic expertise directly with real-world disaster recovery. The coalition brings together an international network of architects, structural engineers, scientists, material specialists, non-profit developers, and financial strategists. Key collaborators and participating institutions include Code Red Consultants, Angel City Lumber, SGH, JPMorgan Chase, Century Housing, Clearinghouse CDFI, Builders FirstSource, Plant Prefab, Sexsmith Architects, Timberlab, ReSAWN, Urban Machine, and TU Delft.

Extending its focus from temporary aid to permanent resilience, the Task Force organized public panels on alternative building materials, mass timber construction, rebuilding economics, and fire-adapted landscaping to help communities make informed, long-term architectural decisions.

Drafting New Material and Spatial Paradigms

The initiative's first year concluded with Architecture After the Fires, a landmark public exhibition showcasing nearly 70 speculative and actionable home design proposals tailored for fire zones. As the largest collection of Wildland-Urban Interface (WUI) architectural proposals to date, the showcase featured ongoing work from prominent practices including Lorcan O'Herlihy, SPF, Studio Murnane, Morphosis, Heather Flood and Story Dimson Studio, Knowhow Shop, and Scott Uriu.

The initiative's first year concluded with Architecture After the Fires, a landmark public exhibition showcasing nearly 70 speculative and actionable home design proposals tailored for fire zones. The Task Force is publishing a comprehensive book documenting these projects, creating a permanent reference guide for building in high-risk environments. Next, SCI-Arc plans to expand its research through institutional partnerships that combine artificial intelligence, environmental risk modeling, and advanced simulation to shape resilient building standards across the region.

India as an AI hedge? The country's stocks could see robust inflows as markets globally tank

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Indian stock market is emerging as an attractive hedge as investors seek refuge from a brutal sell-off in the artificial intelligence trade, making it a strong contender for global capital.

As sentiment around AI sours, foreign investors, who have been underweight on the Indian market for nearly two years due to high valuations, subdued earnings growth and lack of local AI tech champions, could start returning.

"If these foreign long only investor groups were to neutralize their underweight in Indian equities, this could translate into potential net inflows of around $115 billion," Rajiv Batra, head of Asia and co-head of global emerging markets equity strategy at JPMorgan, told CNBC.

He said that multiple factors are currently stacked in favor of the country, such as falling global oil prices, a drop in U.S. Treasury yields, a recovery in earnings growth of Indian companies and unwinding of global AI trade.

"India has emerged as an 'AI hedge' for portfolios trimming or diversifying out of crowded AI trades," Batra said.

Over the last month, the country's benchmark Nifty 50 has gained nearly 9%, outperforming global markets with heavy AI exposure, including the U.S., Japan, South Korea and Taiwan that are deep in the red as investors ditch tech names.

On Friday, as major Asian markets tracked a sell-off on Wall Street overnight, India's benchmark indexes bucked the trend to trade higher. That divergence becomes stark as Japan's Nikkei 225 and Taiwan's Taiex entered bear territory, while South Korea's Kospi deepened its bear market and the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite entered into correction territory overnight.

"India has been dubbed sort of the anti-AI trade, and our view there is that the underlying economic fundamentals have actually been better," Timothy Moe, chief Asia-Pacific equity strategist and co-head of Asia macro research at Goldman Sachs, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" on Friday.

He added that markets like India and Australia that were less exposed to the AI trade will see "some relative outperformance," adding that India's "domestic demand story" gives it an edge between the two.

Unlike Korea and Taiwan, where indexes are heavily concentrated in AI hardware stocks, Indian benchmarks have little exposure to this segment and are instead focused on financial services, consumption, energy, industrials and other services companies.

"India becomes an attractive alternative because of its size, liquidity and relatively lower dependence on the AI cycle," at a time when global investors want to cut exposure to AI-related stocks, Feroze Azeez, joint chief executive at Anand Rathi Wealth, told CNBC.

Foreign investors, which net sold more than $31 billion until October of this year, have turned net buyers over the past four days, investing over $2.3 billion, according to data from NSDL.

This wave of optimism around the Indian markets comes at a time when corporate earnings for the September quarter have shown material improvement.

As per data shared by JPMorgan, earnings growth of listed Indian companies is tracking at more than 20%, and consensus earnings per share estimates have been revised up 0.5% for 2026 and 0.6% for 2027 over the past month.

But despite a steep underperformance of Indian indexes over the last year — even for this year so far the key benchmarks are in the red — and the improvement in earnings growth, its valuations are still relatively higher than other emerging markets.

Global brokerage Bernstein, however, disagrees that the South Asian country should be viewed as an AI hedge.

"India is increasingly being pitched as the hedge to the global AI trade. We disagree. India is neither an AI beneficiary nor an anti-AI trade," it said in a report on Monday, arguing that India's valuations remain expensive.

India is trading at a rolling 12-month forward price-to-earnings of 18.6 times, higher than China's 9.3x and Brazil's 7.9x, the report showed. It is also trading at a premium to AI-heavy markets like South Korea and Taiwan.

"There are easier and cheaper ways to hedge AI than investing in India," Saurabh Mukherjea, founder and chief investment officer of Marcellus Investment Managers, told CNBC. He added, though, that India's earnings growth has been "stellar" for the last two quarters and the valuation of some "quality large caps" is at a decade low.

India has always traded at a premium to the emerging markets of 1.4 times on average since 2007 due to its "durable earnings and a domestically driven growth engine," JPMorgan's Batra said.

The country's markets have struggled since late 2024 due to slowing earnings growth, but that has changed over the last two quarters.

India's near-term appeal was diluted by "high crude, a weak rupee and the availability of high-growth opportunities elsewhere," Hari Shyamsunder, vice president and senior institutional portfolio manager of India equities at Templeton Global Investments, told CNBC.

He pointed out that Nifty now trades below its "10-year average forward P/E," while MSCI India, in October, was at its lowest forward multiple since 2020.

"If crude softens sustainably and growth faces headwinds elsewhere, the picture could change quickly," he said, adding that foreign capital inflows would depend on sustained earnings growth over the next few quarters.

Live: Singapore Airlines Unveils All-New First & Business Class Suites For Its Boeing 777-9

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Last Updated: 12:55 AM EST Nov 5 2026 by Tom Boon

Singapore Airlines has unveiled the next stage in its total refresh of the passenger experience. Today, the Asian airline revealed its new first-class and business-class seats to the world at an event held at the Marina Bay Sands Exhibition Centre in Singapore. The airline will spend more than $2bn SGD ($1.5bn) on the upgrade program.

Airlines around the world are constantly looking to innovate when it comes to passenger experience. With a trophy cabinet of awards, including 2026 SkyTrax Best Airline, Singapore Airlines is looking at the experience in more detail than some others. A cabin refresh that was initially planned to launch with the arrival of the Boeing 777-9 is now expected from H2 next year and will also apply to some of the airline’s Airbus A350-900s.

Singapore Airlines has confirmed that the new Boeing 777-9s will all be delivered with the new cabin experience. Additionally, 41 Airbus A350-900s will get the new cabin. The A350-900 long-haul and A350-900ULR aircraft will get the cabin. However, the A350-900 mid-range aircraft, Boeing 777-300s, Airbus A380s, and all other fleets will not receive the retrofit.

Singapore Airlines CEO, Goh Choon Phong, is welcoming the media and invited guests to Singapore ahead of the reveal of the new Singapore Airlines long-haul and ultra-long-haul cabins. He confirmed that Singapore Airlines will be bringing first class to the world's longest flight between Singapore and New York.

"The A350 ultra-long-range aircraft will also feature the new first-class cabin, and this will be the first time we're featuring first-class on the ultra-long-range planes. So, when you take our flight between Singapore and New York in the future, you will also have the option of going in a first-class cabin."

Singapore Airlines has taken a bold approach with its new business class cabin, designed in collaboration with BMW’s Design Works and RECARO. The new seats are incredibly well thought out from a passenger experience. However, the colour scheme is a departure from the usual Batik-inspired palette that the airline has become famous for.

When describing the new cabin, one of the features consistently pointed out by the Chief Commercial Officer, Lee Lik Hsin, was the fact that passengers sit and sleep facing forward. Work, sleep, dining, and relaxing have all been considered. The seat, for example, has a dedicated space to place your laptop during the meal service, a huge bugbear for many passengers during the meal service. Additionally, the airline will also be introducing doors to its business class seats for the first time.

Arguably the biggest upgrade that Boeing 777 and Airbus A350 passengers will see is the introduction of double beds in select business class seats. According to Singapore Airlines, the full-flat beds measure 79 inches in length and up to 24 inches in width, while a padded bedsheet with memory foam will provide "additional cushioning and support".

When it comes to first class, Singapore Airlines has another bespoke product. Designed with PriestmanGoode, each first class cabin will have six seats, each with a floor to ceiling enclosure. Unlike the business class, the first class cabin sticks to the established Singapore Airlines design language.

The highlight of the first class cabin is the two center seats. These can be combined to make a sofa or double bed. The seats can be synchronised and adjusted as one. Of course, if the passengers occupying these seats are unknown to each other, a full height wall is able to separate them.

Singapore Airlines says that combined, the center seats offer “The largest double bed in commercial aviation”, measuring 80 inches in length and 60 inches in width. Another particular highlight of the seat, built by Collins Aerospace, is the option for face to face dining at all seats, allowing up to four people to dine together in the center seats.

AOI Sales Up 127%, Gross Margin Stuck at 28%: Rapid Earnings Reaction

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Datacenter demand is lifting sales at this optical networking company, but profit is not keeping up. We look at what supports the bottom line and what the outlook requires.

Applied Optoelectronics (AAOI -20.89%) grew third-quarter revenue 127% to $269.9 million, led by datacenter sales. Shares fell about 8% in after-hours trading. Profit is not keeping pace with sales. Gross margin is stuck near 28%, operations still lost money, and the GAAP profit came from other income.

GAAP net income rests on $10.1 million of other income, because operations lost money. The largest piece, a line called "other income (expense), net," gets no explanation in the release.

Gross margin barely moved even as revenue more than doubled. Ramping new transceiver lines usually costs yield, which holds margin down until volumes settle. Guidance for next quarter is 29.5% to 31% on a non-GAAP basis, only a modest step up.

Guidance calls for fourth-quarter revenue about 49% above this quarter at the midpoint. The company also says its 2027 US capacity expansion will take longer, because it is waiting on electrical power and facing other construction issues, though it kept its end-of-2027 target.

Watch whether GAAP operating income turns positive in the fourth quarter as revenue rises toward the guided range. Also track whether the US expansion gets its electrical power in time for the end-of-2027 capacity target.

The Wheel Of Time Universe's True Finale Officially Arrives June 2027

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Wheel of Time fans are still uncertain about the franchise's live-action future after how things played out for the Amazon show. Unfortunately, the series didn't manage to adapt the books well enough to win over Robert Jordan fans, and viewership numbers simply didn't warrant the show's massive budget.

As such, we never got to see how the story would've played out on screen, and certainly never came close to seeing an adaptation of the Wheel of Time's epic conclusion, The Last Battle (or Tarmon Gai'don in the Old Tongue). A proper live-action adaptation of Jordan's complete saga may never happen, though there's renewed hope thanks to iwot Studios' upcoming animated projects, including a new series from Arcane producer Thomas Vu.

Fortunately, fans will finally have the opportunity to experience Wheel of Time's true epic finale for themselves in 2027, thanks to another major release that's already generated serious buzz.

Although Amazon's The Wheel of Time was canceled after just three seasons last year, fans will finally get the opportunity to experience the story's epic conclusion in 2027 with the release of War of the Dragon: The Wheel of Time. Developed by Dire Wolf, the upcoming strategy board game is the first officially licensed tabletop adaptation of Robert Jordan's fantasy saga, drawing from all 14 books and bringing The Last Battle to life in a way the television series never managed to accomplish.

Unlike the Amazon adaptation, which ended long before Rand al'Thor's final confrontation with the Dark One, War of the Dragon covers the entire conflict between the Light and Shadow, culminating in the events of A Memory of Light. Players will take control of opposing factions, recruit familiar characters, command armies, and prepare for Tarmon Gai'don.

The game features two distinct ways to play, including a beginner-friendly Hero Mode and a more expansive Epic Mode. While Hero Mode focuses on the preparations leading up to the final confrontation, Epic Mode actually allows players to experience The Last Battle itself, potentially changing the ending depending on which side emerges victorious.

The release has already a huge hit ahead of its actual launch, with the Kickstarter campaign ending in June after raising an impressive $1,166,993 from 6,156 backers, easily surpassing its original $50,000 funding goal (though these goals are typically set quite low intentionally).

According to the official Kickstarter, backers can expect their copies to arrive in 2027, with June listed as the estimated delivery date. Dire Wolf has clarified that U.S. fulfillment is expected during the first half of 2027, while international orders may take several additional months. The publisher anticipates all backers receiving their games before the end of the year, although manufacturing and shipping timelines remain subject to change.

Fortunately, fans who missed the Kickstarter campaign aren't entirely out of luck. The standard Dragon Reborn Edition is officially scheduled for a retail release in 2027, available through participating local game stores and international distribution partners. A specific retail launch date hasn't been confirmed, though wider availability is expected to follow the initial Kickstarter fulfillment period.

For Wheel of Time fans disappointed by the television adaptation's premature conclusion, War of the Dragon is giving us something truly exciting, which is the opportunity to finally be a part of The Last Battle and determine the fate of Jordan's world for ourselves.

First look: Singapore Airlines shows off its fancy new first and business class

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

It’s been two years since Singapore Airlines began teasing all-new business- and first-class cabins, and nearly 10 years since the last time the airline unveiled a new generation of flagship products.

But, after much anticipation and a flurry of recent announcements about upgrades and refreshes to everything from the airline’s dining program and inflight meals and serviceware to its KrisWorld entertainment system and amenity kits, today we finally got to see what the new Singapore Airlines business- and first-class seats are like along with a preview of the new economy and premium economy seats coming to its Boeing 777-9s.

We joined a slew of international media for two days of announcements and executive interviews culminating in the big reveal at Marina Bay Sands, the even included a newly composed theme song titled "Made of every world you can dream of," performed by flight crew.

“Today Singapore Airlines unveils the next chapter in our end-to-end travel experience,” began Singapore Airlines CEO Goh Choon Phong in his remarks to the crowd. “This is an important milestone and it is also part of our continuous journey to…invest in a product we know our customer will love.”

The new products represent a multiyear investment of over 2 billion Singaporean dollars ($1.56 billion) in addition to the S$130 million ($102 million) the airline has spent renovating its SilverKris lounges around the world.

“This is our biggest investment in improving the customer travel experience,” said Siva Govindasamy, divisional vice president of public affairs, “because we’re not just talking about seat development. We’re talking about improvements in the customer service journey from end to end.”

The airline surveyed over 5,000 frequent flyers, incorporating their feedback on what they were looking for from the travel experience, what elements of Singapore Airlines’ current seats and service they liked, and what needed to evolve.

“Our development process has been shaped by extensive research and feedback to help us understand how our customers work, dine, relax, sleep and use technology in their journeys,” explained Lee Lik Hsin, Singapore Airlines’ chief commercial officer, to a small media gathering ahead of the event. “This launch represents a significant milestone, but not an end point,” he continued.

Here’s how, based on what we saw of the new seats and service elements, and all the fancy, industry-leading features in store for passengers.

Related: Singapore KrisFlyer: Ultimate guide to earning, redeeming and elite status

Perhaps the most exciting announcement of the bunch was the unveiling of Singapore Airlines’ new long-haul first-class seats.

Designed by PriestmanGoode and manufactured by Collins Aerospace, these fixtures were initially slated for the airline’s upcoming order of Boeing 777-9s, but given the delays on those aircraft deliveries, Singapore is also installing them on its ultra-long-range subfleet of seven Airbus A350-900ULRs starting in the second half of 2027. This will be the first time the airline’s A350s have sported a first-class cabin.

The A350s will have just four of these seats on board in a 1-2-1 configuration at the very front of the plane. The 777-9s will eventually have six of them on board — two running down either side of the cabin and two in the center. They have 89 1/2 inches of pitch, giving them a fairly large footprint, though not as big as the Suites aboard the airline’s Airbus A380s. Singapore Airlines does not have plans to update the first-class cabins on board its Boeing 777-300s or A380s.

While the new side seats are cocoons unto themselves, the center seats were conceived as a shared experience for travel companions, who can lower the privacy divider and armrests to create a sort of sofa that then transforms into what the airline is dubbing the largest double bed in the sky, at 80 inches long and 60 inches wide.

In one of the lighter moments during the pre-reveal press conferences, one journalist asked Singapore’s Hsin how flight crew would address couples who might want to use the space inappropriately. Without missing a beat, Hsin replied, “We will address it,” prompting chuckles around the room. “We want every passenger to be able to enjoy the comfort that we have provided without disturbing the comfort and experience for any other customer.”

Back to the seat, though, it incorporates a cocktail tray for inflight aperitifs and wardrobe areas on the aisles where passengers can stow their clothes (after slipping into the airline’s new pajamas, of course…more on that below!). Those with a keen eye will also notice a subtle batik motif on the soft wall panels hearkening to the airline’s famous uniforms and branding.

The chairs are upholstered in Poltrona Frau leather, are 23 inches wide (30 with the armrests lowered) and recline to 80-inch lie-flat beds that flight attendants will make up with 100% cotton linens, a duvet and a selection of pillows, as well as a breathable new mattress topper from German luxury brand Paradies.

Passengers can adjust the various components of the seat including the arm and legrests, the ottoman and lumbar support as well as the lighting using the inflight entertainment system control and can create a profile so that their settings will carry over to their next flight.

The palette includes a mix of taupes, browns, silvers and warm terracotta-tinged accents meant to telegraph a feeling of luxury, harmony, privacy and warmth according to PriestmanGoode director Ben Rowan.

Passengers can dine face to face thanks to large, sturdy tray tables and will find a wireless charging pad, USB-C ports and a universal AC power port to keep their devices charged. The 777-9 seats will have a USB-A port as well.

The storage areas include a slim personal wardrobe, a vanity drawer lined in leather and a small compartment for a cabin bag beneath the ottoman.

The inflight entertainment system is a 32-inch 4K touchscreen loaded with the airline’s KrisWorld content system. It will include an integrated touch panel display for flight information, destination weather, inflight Wi-Fi connectivity status and the availability of lavatories.

Passengers will be given Bang & Olufsen wireless headphones to use, but will also be able to connect to the entertainment system using their own Bluetooth headsets.

Overall, the new first-class seats are a big step forward from the first-class cabins you’ll find aboard the airline’s Boeing 777-300s and sit somewhere between those and the A380 Suites. Their look feels like an evolution of the palette and finishes of the Suites, with welcome tech updates that should keep travelers happy and connected.

Related: The ultimate guide to Singapore Suites

Lest you think first class is the biggest news, the airline also unveiled its all-new business-class seats, designed from the ground up in conjunction with seat manufacturer Recaro and BMW’s DesignWorks.

These seats will appear on the Boeing 777-9 and both the A350-900ULR and the long-haul A350-900 but not the airline’s medium-haul A350s. If you’re losing track, don’t worry, we have tables laying out the details below. We also got a few exclusive shots from a mockup cabin at Singapore Airlines' headquarters the day before the official unveiling, which you can see below. Just note that they don't have all the finishes and other details the final seats on board will have.

Catching up with the current airline industry trends, the airline has decided on suite-style pods with closing doors for privacy. The doors are decorated with a stylized “satellite” stencil, and you’ll see this pattern in the stitching on the leather seats as well.

“We wanted to bring an automotive-inspired seat and our knowledge from experiential design into the cabin,” explained BMW DesignWorks CEO Julia de Bono. Thus the seats feel like a mix between seats in a luxury sedan and a racecar with mineral gray-toned leather and bright yellow trim, meant to convey balance and stability on the one hand and energy and creativity on the other, according to de Bono.

The seats are laid out in a front-facing 1-2-1 pattern — this was a direct result of customer feedback, with frequent flyers telling airline executives that they wanted to move past an angled orientation, which could feel constraining at times.

The seats have 50 inches of pitch and are up to 24 inches wide and recline to 79-inch lie-flat beds — a departure from the current A350 lie-flats, which flight attendants have to flip over in order to get them into bed mode. Some center seats can be configured as double beds when the privacy dividers are lowered, which should be exciting for folks who tend to travel with a companion.

Passengers will also receive new padded seat covers with memory foam for cushioning and support.

The tray table placement and stowage — it pops out from under the entertainment screen and can be maneuvered flexibly — also allows the seat to have a larger ottoman, alleviating a common complaint about the airline’s current business-class seats.

Like the new first-class seats, these will allow passengers to create a profile via the inflight entertainment system so their preferred seat and lighting settings are saved from flight to flight.

They have 22-inch 4K touchscreen entertainment systems (20 inches on the 777-9) with Bluetooth pairing capability. Among the storage spaces are a small cubby that slides out from the chair-side seatback and a little cupboard next to the entertainment screen. Passengers will also find wireless charging pads, USB-C ports and universal power plugs.

Related: Which Singapore Airlines business class is better? A380 vs A350

Singapore Airlines worked with FactoryDesign and Collins Aerospace to create updated versions of its economy and premium economy seats specifically for the Boeing 777-9. There are currently no plans for these to be installed aboard the airline’s other jets.

The new premium economy seats have a swanky, contoured look with dark gray leather upholstery and vibrant orange accents. They will see their seat pitch increase from the 38 inches previous versions offer to 40 inches and they will get an extra inch of recline, rounding it up to nine instead of eight. They will be 19 inches wide and have adjustable leg and footrests plus adjustable headsets.

Their entertainment screens will be 16 inches wide with 4K resolution and Bluetooth pairing capability. The seats will also feature universal power plugs, USB-C ports and USB-A ports on some aircraft.

As for economy seating, these look a lot like current versions and are squeezed into a tight 3-4-3 layout, with just 17.6 inches of width per seat. Their screens get an upgrade to 13 inches and 4K resolution with Bluetooth pairing available. They will also have USB-C ports (and A versions on some aircraft) plus universal power plugs.

Related: Worth the upcharge? Flying Singapore Airlines premium economy

So how exactly are all these new seats going to get onto planes and when will passengers start seeing them? Here’s the plan.

Singapore Airlines currently has 58 Airbus A350s. Seventeen of those are configured for medium-haul and regional routes.

Of the remaining 41, 34 are long-range A350-900s with business class, premium economy and economy on board. These will be kitted out with new business class seats with the following adjusted seat numbers in each class. The process is expected to start in the second half of 2027 and to last through 2030.

The other seven A350s are the airline’s A350-900ULRs, which stands for Ultra-Long-Range, and which are deployed on the airline’s longest routes from Singapore Changi Airport (SIN) to New York’s John F. Kennedy International Airport (JFK) and Newark Liberty International Airport (EWR). These ULR jets will get a full overhaul with the new first- and business-class seats. The changes will reduce the plane's overall seating capacity to 132, down from 161.

All the seats on this jet will be new, but here’s how it breaks down.

So the good news is that the majority of Singapore Airlines' long-haul fleet will have new first- and business-class seats on them once this retrofit is complete and the 777-9s are in service. The bad news is that we don't know how long that will take, exactly, and we don't know what routes will see the new seats first.

In the meantime, here are some of the other fun things the airline has cooking up. Quite literally, in some cases.

Related: A review of Singapore Suites first class from Frankfurt to New York

As if new seats weren’t enough, Singapore Airlines is making major updates to its meal service as well.

The airline is already well-known for offering a “Book the Cook” meal preorder service with dozens of dishes available to flyers in Suites, first, business and premium economy, but there are about to be a lot more options.

The airline is touting over 100 new and refreshed dishes on offer, including appetizers, mains and desserts, which will be introduced across all four classes of service under a new category titled “SIA Chefs’ Selection” developed by the airline’s partner guest chefs, who include Monica Galetti, Nandu Jubany, Sanjeev Kapoor, Sid Sahrawat, Yoshihiro Murata and Zhu Jun.

Among the new dishes you might spot are poached Maine lobster with champagne sauce in Suites and first class, a Japanese-inspired prawn cocktail in business class, beef short rib in coconut-based sambal percik with nasi kerabu rice in business class, tandoor-roasted paneer in tangy pasanda gravy in all classes and Irish lamb stew with buttered cabbage and caraway seeds in premium economy.

And take a closer look at what those dishes will be served on in business and first. The airline is introducing an all-new line of chinaware from French luxury brand Christofle, making it the first airline to partner with the longstanding luxury house.

The patterns are inspired by Christofle’s Malmaison Impériale collection and incorporate an interpretation of Singapore Airline’s floral batik patterns. There will also be new custom-designed Christofle glassware with tulip-shaped champagne flutes and wine glasses with long stems for more utility.

A new selection of spirits across the board and new wines for business and first class passengers is coming, too. First-class and Suites passengers will be treated to an expanded selection of champagnes including Krug Grande Cuvée, Louis Roederer Cristal, Taittinger Comtes de Champagne, Charles Heidsieck Blanc des Millénaires and Bollinger La Grande Année while those in business class will find Charles Heidsieck Brut Reserve N.V. and Piper-Heidsieck Vintage.

Among the new spirits to be carried on board, there will be a blended whiskey from Japan’s Komoro Distillery created exclusively for Singapore Airlines first class, Bowmore 19 Year Old Pinot Noir Cask Finish single-malt Scotch whisky and Roky Botanical Kasane Edition gin in business class and Whyte & Mackay blended Scotch whisky in premium economy and economy.

Related: Behind the scenes with Singapore Airlines' wine experts as they choose the Champagnes that will be on board soon

For a carrier with such a premium reputation, one thing that surprises some business-class passengers is the fact that Singapore Airlines has not traditionally handed out amenities in business class (first-class passengers do get them, though). That’s all about to change, starting this week.

In fact, Singapore Airlines is introducing all-new amenity kits available on flights over six hours. Here’s what’s in store for passengers.

When Singapore Airlines launched its second-generation A380 First Class Suites in 2017, it also formed a partnership with French luxury house Lalique to create onboard amenities including pajamas, linens and amenity kits with fragrances and skincare products.

Now, with the introduction of its third-generation suites, the airline is turning to French cosmetics brand Sisley to create all-new men’s and women’s kits for first class passengers featuring anti-aging Sisleÿa (for women) and Sisleÿum (for men) products.

Passengers will also find Sisley’s Eau de Campagne fragrance, moisturizing body lotion and floral spray mist along with dental kits in the first-class lavatories.

As for pajamas, customers will receive all-new sleeper suits in dark gray cotton with a subtle imprint of the airline’s batik pattern on the plackets and side seams, along with new, better padded slippers. They will be available in four sizes.

While visiting the airline’s fancy The Private Room lounge and the First Class SilverKris Lounge at Changi Terminals 2 and 3, passengers will also find Hair Rituel by Sisley fortifying densifying shampoo and restructuring conditioner in the shower suites, along with Sisley’s Eau de Campagne bath and shower gel, fragrance and moisturizing body lotion.

Singapore Airlines is also introducing new amenity kits and, for the first time, pajamas for business-class passengers.

The sleeping apparel will be navy-blue poly-cotton with a subtle batik print on the tops’ inner collars and the lining of the trousers’ pockets. They will be available in two sizes.

Business-class passengers will also receive new amenity kits from Swedish brand Byredo that include:

The business-class lavatories will have Blanche body lotion and vitamin E and chamomile facial toner.

Don’t worry, the airline hasn’t forgotten about premium economy passengers, who pay a pretty penny for that extra legroom. They will now receive new zip pouches made from recycled materials and decorated with the airline’s recognizable batik motif.

The pouches will contain Australian personal care brand Ink & Water’s white tea-scented lip balm as well as eye masks, socks and dental kits.

Related: Singapore Airlines premium economy review

Even the folks in the back will receive drawstring pouches with a batik motif containing eye masks, socks and a dental kit.

Long flights are tough on kids, so for travelers 12 years of age and under on flights that are over five hours long, the airline will offer collectible Beary soft toys — essentially airline-branded teddy bears. The airline also offers Pixar’s “Toy Story”-themed amenities on all flights including things like infant bibs, activity pads for kids and drawstring bags.

Related: I make my own amenity kit for long-haul flights: Here’s what’s inside

Singapore Airlines will also be updating the KrisWorld inflight entertainment systems on its A350s and offering new systems on its forthcoming 777-9s.

The new systems will have 4K resolution and Bluetooth pairing capability, but interface improvements that include better search functions, content shortcuts, a mini-player function so passengers can browse content while watching something, better personalization and wellness programming.

First-class passengers will receive Bang & Olufsen noise-cancelling Bluetooth headsets to use in flight while business-class and premium economy flyers will receive active noise-cancellation sets. Economy passengers get wired headphones, but all will be able to pair their own Bluetooth devices with the new systems.

The airline will also progressively roll out new Starlink Wi-Fi service starting in 2027 aboard its Airbus A380s, A350-900 long-haul fleet and its A350-900ULRs. Like the airline’s current Wi-Fi offerings, Starlink service will be free for business and first class passengers and passengers in all cabins who are members of KrisFlyer or PPS Club.

“For our customers, every touchpoint — from seats, meals, inflight entertainment, connectivity and amenities, to our lounges and other ground experiences — is part of one similar Singapore Airlines journey," remarked Phong in his speech at the unveiling event. "By continuing to invest and elevate this end-to-end customer experience, we offer greater comfort and convenience while reinforcing our commitment to earning their trust and loyalty at every single phase of their journey.”

New airline seats represent huge investments of time, money and research, let alone the costs of updating onboard services and amenities. That makes Singapore Airlines’ current slate of announcements all the more exciting.

Flyers have a lot to look forward to on board the airline’s long-haul A350s and forthcoming 777-9s. While the first-class seats are not as large as the Suites on board the airline’s A380s, they are still roomy with high-end finishes and up-to-date tech that passengers should welcome.

The business-class seats represent the largest departure for the airline, with a new front-facing configuration and what feels like a totally new color palette that might jarring for some longtime flyers. Thoughtful ergonomic features in terms of stowage and the tray table and closing privacy doors are real pluses, though.

As for premium economy and economy, hopefully the airline will consider bringing its latest seats to the A350 as well in an attempt to standardize the experience across the fleet. Still, for 777-9 passengers, these updates will be a win.

If you're curious to see some of the elements unveiled today for yourself, there should be a special display at The Jewel at Changi in the coming weeks followed by one in the transit area of Terminal 3 in early 2027.

The Fitbit Edge’s onboard GPS is a battery hog — but I found an easy fix

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

You can more than triple the Edge's GPS battery life without sacrificing accuracy

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The new Fitbit Edge features onboard GPS, which is a very good thing for folks who enjoy outdoor workouts, and also just one of the many reasons it's one of the best fitness trackers you can buy.

Unlike the more entry-level Fitbit Air, you can accurately log location, distance, speed, pace, climb, and more with the Edge without needing to carry a paired smartphone.

There is one downside to the Fitbit Edge's onboard GPS though: it uses a lot of battery. Just to be clear, the wearable's overall battery life is pretty solid; I averaged about a week of use per charge while writing the Fitbit Edge review.

However, when using the Edge's onboard GPS for location tracking, it burns through roughly 12% battery per hour. This translates to just over eight hours of use before it's lights out.

Fortunately, there are a few ways to minimize battery drain without sacrificing location tracking insights. Both can be found in the Google Health app under Devices > Fitbit Edge > Device preferences > GPS settings.

Here you can switch location tracking from built-in to either dynamic — which uses both your smartphone and the onboard antenna — or smartphone-only.

The disadvantage of switching off the onboard GPS, of course, is that you now need to haul your smartphone along. But then again, if you're logging an eight-plus-hour workout, it may not be a bad idea to carry a means of communication just in case of emergencies.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Battery drain after one hour of workout tracking

More crucially, the non-onboard GPS options significantly reduce battery consumption in my testing. For example, switching to dynamic GPS increases battery life from around eight hours to closer to 25 hours for outdoor workouts.

But does turning off the onboard GPS negatively impact tracking accuracy? The Google Health app seems to suggest that it might, noting that the built-in option offers "high accuracy" while the dynamic one "automatically switches between devices to optimize battery and accuracy." Hmmm..

To find out if there's really a difference in tracking accuracy between the three GPS modes, I logged three 30-minute walking workouts using each one. As a control, I also logged the walks using my reliable Garmin Forerunner 170 Music, worn on the opposite wrist.

In my first test using only onboard GPS, the Fitbit Edge delivered impressively similar data to the control, including a step count total that's off by only one.

When using the Fitbit Edge's dynamic GPS mode, which switches between the onboard and smartphone-based antennas, the Edge once again logged data that falls remarkably well in line with the control across the board.

Finally, let's see how the Fitbit Edge performs in smartphone GPS-only mode.

With the exception of somewhat sluggish pace data, the Fitbit Edge's smartphone-only GPS tracking mode is also right on the money in terms of step count, distance, and climb data.

Ultimately, all three Fitbit Edge GPS modes delivered accurate-enough results that I'd confidently rely on any of them going forward, but is one definitively the best? That's going to truly come down to the user.

For example, I plan to set and leave the Edge's built-in tracking switched on unless a particularly long hike or multi-day trek is in my future, in which case, dynamic mode will be my preference. Actually, in that scenario, I'd probably opt for something more rugged, like the Garmin Instinct 3, to log my efforts instead.

But for everyday jogs, bike rides, dog walks, urban hikes, and other casual, quick workouts where I don't want the bulk of a smartphone weighing me down, you can bet I'll be sticking with the onboard GPS.

Elf Beauty CEO on Gen Alpha, Record Sales and Cracking the US

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

After joining a call with Vogue Business, ahead of Elf Beauty’s Q2 earnings, it’s not long before CEO Tarang Amin mentions that this quarter marks the company’s 31st consecutive quarter of net sales growth.

For Q2 2027, net sales rose 51% to $520.8 million, bolstered by retailer and e-commerce channels across the US and the globe. Gross margin grew 160 basis points to 71%, and adjusted EBITDA reached $123.4 million, up 86% year on year. The company, which comprises Elf, Rhode, Naturium and Well People, also updated its guidance for fiscal 2027, predicting net sales of $2 billion, up from $1.9 billion. While the company did not share a financial breakdown by brand, Amin tells Vogue Business that every brand grew. “It was a phenomenal quarter for us,” he says.

Amin assumed his role in 2014, and since 2020, Elf Beauty has acquired Well People, Naturium, and most recently, Rhode (for $1 billion) in a bullish M&A sweep. During the summer, the Elf brand expanded into haircare, launching Elf Hair with a suite of products including a moisturizing shampoo and conditioner, an anti-frizz styling spray, and treatment oil. (Elf’s own brands also include Elf Cosmetics and Elf Skin.)

Amin’s moves could reap a return on investment. According to Euromonitor, the global haircare category is forecast to grow 24% by 2030, reaching £129 billion, while outpacing the growth of established beauty verticals like makeup and fragrance (both in the double digits).

Despite geopolitical uncertainty and inflation softening consumer sentiment, US shoppers are still buying beauty, especially online. This is a win for Elf, as a US-based “masstige” (mass but prestige-positioned) company that took on TikTok Shop before most competitors, and has captured millennial, Gen Z and Gen Alpha shoppers with its affordability, a key concern for shoppers in the US, as we saw with the “blue tsunami” mid-term results this week.

After cracking Gen Z and millennials, Elf Beauty is eyeing up Gen Alpha.

At the same time, Elf Beauty — available online globally — is expanding its retail footprint in a bid to diversify revenue and scale. In fiscal 2025, Elf Beauty took on the Netherlands and Belgium, as well as Poland and Germany through partner Rossmann. The new territories joined the US, UK, Mexico, and Canada, where partners such as Walmart, Target, and Sephora have supported the brand. Then, in September this year, Rhode launched with Sephora in Europe, bringing the brand to more than 500 stores across 19 new markets. Elf Beauty claims that this has boosted quarterly results.

As analysts and beauty retailers take stock, Amin shares his next steps.

CEO Tarang Amin joined the company in 2014.

Vogue: Elf showed strong growth for Q1 2027. What led to this uplift?

Well, we’ve been known for our value proposition for 22 years. And at a time when consumers are more concerned about affordability, our brands shine. It’s not only resonating now, but will continue to resonate as we go forward. I’d say the second thing for us is that we’re in an incredible category where consumers really care about innovation. We’re known for our innovation, taking inspiration from prestige and putting our Elf twist on it, and bringing it [out] at great value. Most important is our unique ability to engage our community. We were one of the first brands on TikTok. We have our own Twitch channel focused on female empowerment. We have the number one branded experience on Roblox. We live with our community, and we give them what they want. It’s made us the number one brand amongst Gen Z, Gen Alpha, and millennials.

Vogue: You mentioned Gen Alpha, which has been a strong cohort for Elf for a while. As consumers, are Gen Alpha similar to, or completely different from, other generations?

We’re seeing more similarities than dissimilarities. They’re digitally savvy consumers, so that [bodes] well [with] us leaning into connected commerce. We’re [a bestseller] on Amazon [and] on TikTok Shop. They are also seeking value. Then also, the way we engage with them is not about selling cosmetics or skincare. It’s engaging with them the way they want to engage. They also care about the values that a company stands for. We are double certified, cruelty-free — by Peta and Leaping Bunny. We are vegan, and we’re the only beauty company that’s Fair Trade certified.

Vogue: How do you intend to grow while maintaining that commitment to quality?

We have tremendous white space. As great as our results have been, only 25% of our business is outside of the US, compared to many at 70%. There’s already pent-up demand for our brands around the world. Sixty-five percent of Elf’s social [following] is outside the US; 75% of Rhode’s social [following] is outside the US. Just a few weeks ago, we launched Rhode with Sephora in Europe, [across] 19 countries. It was Sephora’s biggest launch ever. It beat our launch with Sephora in North America.

The other thing we’re seeing is our brands are highly elastic. Consumers want to see this same prestige quality and incredible prices in other categories. Just a few years ago, we [launched] skincare. Elf Skin is already over a $200 million retail business. And more recently, we entered the haircare category — same concept, where we have a curated six-product line-up, all with very clear frames of reference to prestige [products] but all $10 or under. Just a few weeks ago, we entered the fragrance category with Elf Pop, a line of fragrance and body products. That elasticity of our brands gives us multiple vectors of growth.

Elf Hair launched this year, following the official skincare rollout in 2022.

Vogue: What drew you to haircare, and how did you first notice momentum?

We’ve been hearing hair requests from our community for years. There isn’t a category they don’t want to see Elf in. We have incredible partnerships with our customers around the world. Target is our longest-standing national retail partner in the US. They’re the ones who came to us with Elf Skin, saying, “We think there’s a huge opportunity. You’re the number one brand in face. You should go into skin.” That was a great insight for us. They came to us with Elf Hair as well, saying, “You know, much of the best innovation is actually happening on the prestige side, which isn’t accessible to most consumers.” In the US alone, two-thirds live paycheck to paycheck.

But it’s also a disciplined rollout strategy. I mean, we could probably go to any category we wanted to, but we take our time and really make sure that we’re nurturing these categories. I’m happy that just in the first three months alone at Target, we’re already a top 20 haircare brand with them.

Vogue: Going into the new year, what are your plans for your social media strategy?

I think one of the things that really differentiates us is that we don’t follow a typical beauty playbook in terms of how people go and try to convince people to buy products. We live where our community lives, and we engage them in a way they want to. We just launched our second album. I don’t know another company that is into music, that’s into women’s sports, that has the number one branded experience on Roblox, our own channel on Twitch, and so it’s a much broader view of how we’re engaging with our community. It goes well beyond beauty.

Vogue: Would you ever enter the prestige segment?

For us, accessibility is always going to be one of the hallmarks of the Elf Beauty company. So if you take a look at Elf Color Cosmetics, the average unit retail is about $7 in the US. Elf Skin is about $10. If you look at our Naturium brands, the average unit retail is about $18, and our Rhode brand is about $25. All are incredibly accessible relative to the quality of the products that they deliver. Usually, a similar product would be multiple times higher, and so I’d say accessibility will always be an important element. We can do entry-level prestige as long as it continues to offer great value. I don’t see us going into luxury. I think that’s a different business model. We’re really about democratizing access.

Vogue: You’ve stepped further into Europe. Are you keen to expand in other regions too?

I’d say the whole world is open to us. I mean, just in the last quarter alone, not only did we launch throughout Europe, but our Elf launch in Sephora Brazil was the number one brand that they carry in both dollars and units, which built on the number one position we have with Elf in Mexico. We’re in India with both Nykaa and Amazon, and have a very fast-growing business there. For us, it’s about executing with excellence.

Vogue: We’ve seen the midterm election results come in. How do you feel that will affect your business?

I would say the biggest theme from this election was affordability, and when it comes to affordability, I believe we’re the best positioned of any company — just given our track record. We’re for every eyelid and face, and so long as we continue to deliver that superior value, I feel quite confident about our business.

“I would have been angry in their place”: Julián Álvarez asks forgiveness from Atlético de Madrid!

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

What if the story between Julián Álvarez and Atlético de Madrid started again? It’s still early to know, but this week could mark a turning point in the Argentine center forward’s season with the Colchoneros. After announcing his desires elsewhere in the middle of the World Cup, the 2022 World champion was engaged in an intense standoff with Madrid management, who refused to let him go, whatever the price. Finally remaining at Atlético, Álvarez was taken in by his supporters on his return to the Metropolitano, they who had not forgotten his words. The whistles and rejection from supporters added to the difficult start to the season for the former Manchester City player, between irregular performances and a muscle injury.

But these last seven days could mark the beginning of his redemption. Decisive last weekend on the pitch of Espanyol Barcelona (0-1), then scorer in the Champions League in the middle of the week in Stuttgart (1-1), Álvarez gave the Madrilenians victory this Saturday in La Liga, by scoring the only goal of the match against Deportivo La Coruña on a serve from Grimaldo (1-0). A first achievement in the league this season, warmly welcomed by his teammates and supporters. The evening could have ended there for the player trained at River Plate, but he chose to speak at the microphone of Movistar+ in order to send his most sincere apologies to Atlético fans after the eventful summer period.

“ I wanted to apologize to our supporters for the comments I made during the World Cup. It was a mistake, both in how I phrased them and when I said them. Especially since I understand why the supporters were angry. I would have been angry myself if I had been in their place. Even more so when you see how much they supported me. They were very generous, and I am truly grateful to them. I would also like to thank the team, as they supported me throughout this period “, he explained first.

“ I feel better and better. I’m grateful to all my teammates because they supported me. I work to improve myself. I have been feeling better for a few matches, both physically and in terms of play.he continued. I want to help the team and give the best of myself. When you feel good in life, it shows on the pitch. Even though we are professionals, everything has an influence. I feel good and ready to fight for everything. I hope that through hard work and humility I can turn things around. » Words that could help ease tensions and begin a new start for Álvarez, under contract until 2030 with Atlético.

Recent games made in Unity include Big Walk, Endless Legend 2, and Agent 64: Spies Never Die.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Earlier this year, Electronic Arts was snapped up by a consortium including Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund (PIF) and the investment firms Silver Lake and Affinity Partners in a deal worth $55 billion. According to Reuters, now the PIF and Silver Lake have their sights set on Unity Software, the company behind the multiplatform game engine of the same name.

Silver Lake is already one of Unity's bigger shareholders, owning roughly 8% of the company since investing around $400 million in it back in 2017. "Silver ​Lake’s Egon Durban also sits ⁠on Unity's board," as Reuters notes, "a dual role that would typically lead him to recuse himself from board deliberations on a sale."

The deliberations so far apparently include a valuation of about $25 billion, though since then Unity's most recent earnings report has come in, showing profits higher than estimated. Unity's come a long way since the Runtime Fee scandal, but it's not all about improvements to the engine like its ability to integrate with Unreal. As Reuters reports, "Strong ⁠growth in Unity's AI-powered Vector advertising platform helped it post its first quarterly profit on a GAAP basis since going public in September 2020."

The first thing that happened to EA after it was acquired was mass layoffs and being saddled with $18 billion of debt. I can't imagine anyone actually working at Unity has their fingers crossed for this deal to go through, but the videogame industry does seem determined to speedrun its way into a consolidation crisis where three consortiums own everything and almost everyone is unemployed.

Justin Jefferson says his frustration came from "wanting to win"

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Vikings receiver Justin Jefferson engaged in a rare display of frustration late in Sunday’s 19-15 loss to the Lions. He also didn’t talk to reporters after the game, possibly to avoid saying things that would underscore what he was feeling in the moment.

Four days later, Jefferson spoke to the media — and the No. 1 topic was his apparent mood during Minnesota’s first loss of the season.

“It was wanting to win,” Jefferson said, via Kevin Seifert of ESPN, “and I felt like we had plenty of opportunities to win that game and we only hurt ourselves in multiple ways. It’s just wanting better from the whole entire offense especially. And I feel like the potential and the guys that we have in that room is something very, very special. I feel like we’re wasting that if not going out there executing the plays that we have called and just not living to that standard that we feel like we live up to every single day.”

Jefferson, who always says all the right things about his quarterbacks, said all the right things about Kyler Murray, whose performance against Detroit largely contributed to the failure to score more than 15 points.

“I feel like Kyler has continued to grow,” Jefferson said. “I feel like Kyler is continuing to get comfortable with this offense. And I feel like the plays that are there are plays that he’s well capable of making. And those are plays that we’ve seen him make multiple times throughout the season, throughout practice and in training camp as well. So we definitely have faith in him. We have confidence in him to go out there and be that guy that we need him and want him to be.

“But all in all, it’s just executing our plays and just coming ready to play. And I feel like once he’s back out there and he gets more comfortable with the system, more comfortable with just throwing to these different type of receivers, then I feel like he’s going to look a lot better in [reporters’] eyes. But we see that every single day. We see that type of Kyler that we need out there on Sundays every single day. So just bringing that on Sundays. We’re behind him. We’re confident in him, so we just going to keep riding with him.”

For now, the Vikings will ride with Carson Wentz, while Murray is out with a hamstring injury. And Wentz has proven that, of all the quarterbacks who have played for the Vikings since Sam Darnold left for Seattle after the 2024 season, Wentz knows how to get the ball to Jefferson.

Soft bench or not, Wentz has an opportunity to stay on the field if he makes the plays that Murray didn’t on Sunday. Plays that, by the time the game was on the line, coach Kevin O’Connell didn’t allow Murray to try to make.

The one moment that resonates from Sunday’s game wasn’t anything Murray did or failed to do. It came from O’Connell’s decision to punt from the Minnesota 33-yard line on fourth and five with 3:01 to play. Given the manner in which the Lions were running the ball, the likelihood of getting another opportunity to score a touchdown was slim.

Put simply, if O’Connell didn’t trust Murray to gain five yards with the game on the line, O’Connell shouldn’t entrust the starting job to Murray.

For now, Murray technically can’t play due to injury. It’s hard not to wonder whether, if Murray had made the plays needed to outscore the Lions on Sunday, he’d be playing on Monday night despite the injured hamstring.

Cloudflare Sales Up 39%, Gross Margin Falls: Rapid Earnings Reaction

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Cloudflare is growing quickly, and the release shows where the cost of that growth landed. We look at margins, cash flow, and what the GAAP numbers add.

Cloudflare (NET -8.28%) grew third-quarter revenue 39% to $782.5 million. Shares jumped about 15% in after-hours trading. Growth is strong, and each new dollar of sales now leaves less behind. Gross margin fell about 3 points, and free cash flow margin slipped to 10% from 13%.

Cost of revenue grew 54%, far faster than sales, which alone explains the lower gross margin. The release does not say why. Cloudflare runs its own global network, so added capacity hits cost of revenue and capital spending before the sales that fill it.

Equipment purchases rose 44%, faster than operating cash flow, so free cash flow grew far less than sales.

Non-GAAP operating margin improved, but GAAP shows a wider loss. The gap is mostly stock-based pay and related payroll taxes, which rose 45%, faster than revenue.

Watch whether gross margin stabilizes as revenue heads toward the $845 million to $846 million fourth-quarter guide. Also look for the cost and headcount of the workforce reduction plan, which the release mentions only in its forward-looking statements.

Researchers hung baited cameras off a boat and identified 50 different oceanic whitetip sharks using only their fins

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Baited cameras off Cat Island, The Bahamas, filmed 50 oceanic whitetip sharks that researchers told apart by dorsal fin markings over two seasons.

In late April 2023, a Florida International University (FIU) crew off Cat Island in The Bahamas started dropping baited GoPro cameras over the side. The goal was to film oceanic whitetip sharks about 43 feet (13 meters) down, with 2 pounds (1 kilogram) of bonito as bait.

They came back in 2024 and did it again. By then the video showed 50 separate whitetips. Each one was picked out by the notches, shape and pigmentation pattern of its first dorsal fin, the tall fin on the back.

Candace Fields, who is Bahamian and got her PhD in marine biology at FIU in 2025, ran the survey. Counting fins sometimes put up to three times as many sharks at a camera as the usual tally. All of it, though, comes from one island and two springs of fieldwork.

According to NOAA Fisheries, oceanic whitetips can reach 11 feet (3.4 meters). They spend most of their time in the top 656 feet (200 meters) of the open ocean.

NOAA puts the drop across the Pacific at about 80 to 95 percent since the mid-1990s. It names bycatch in commercial fisheries and demand for the fins as the main threat, and says some recent recovery is possible in the Pacific.

A Mote Marine Laboratory report found that most species in the shark fin trade are threatened with extinction. The IUCN lists the oceanic whitetip as Critically Endangered, and the U.S. Endangered Species Act lists it as threatened.

Yannis Papastamatiou is an FIU marine biologist at the university’s Institute of Environment and one of Fields’ advisers. “When you’re talking about marine predators in the ocean, that’s very difficult to answer, much harder than with terrestrial predators,” he told FIU News.

“If you want an idea of the abundance of lions, you can do airplane surveys and count them. But in the marine environment, you can’t see things, so that’s very difficult to do.”

FIU researchers had already surveyed another shark species for 10 years at an atoll with baited cameras, FIU News reported. Off Cat Island the water was too deep to anchor cameras to the bottom.

Fields tried two kinds of rig in late April and early May of 2023 and 2024. One was a frame of 3/4-inch galvanized steel pipe holding at least two GoPro Hero9 Black cameras. It drifted on a rope under a float and a flagged marker buoy, with a dive weight underneath.

The other was a wire bait cage with a camera. It hung about 49 feet (15 meters) from a 79-foot (24-meter) Viking sportfishing yacht. In the acknowledgments, the authors thank Captain Scott Genereux for running the Lady G.

The crew put out 99 sets between Columbus Point and Tartar Bank, a shallow seamount west of the island. Of those, 76 hung from the boat and 23 drifted, each at least half a mile (800 meters) from the last. Whitetips came to 51 of them.

Some earlier accounts said whitetips home in on boat engines. In this survey the tethered cage drew no more sharks than the drifting rig. Whether the engine was on or off did not matter either.

More sharks turned up at Columbus Point, a shelf edge where tuna, wahoo and billfish feed in winter and spring. Windy days brought more of them too. The authors think the rigs drifted farther and the scent of bonito spread wider.

Fields told FIU News that reading whitetip fins is similar to reading the spots on a whale shark. In the Western Indian Ocean, photos of those spots helped track individual whale sharks over ten years.

Until now, whitetip photo ID came from divers, who could swim into position for a clean shot. “In a video survey, obviously, you have no means to control how the shark approaches the camera. So, one big question was: is this even viable?” Fields said.

Each time a whitetip came into view, an annotator saved a still of its first dorsal fin. A shark got its own code, such as OWT1, only once both sides of the fin were on camera.

Some sharks circled the rig again and again. Others swam in at angles that hid the markings, and glare and murky water hid more. Still, the team picked out at least one shark by its fin on 41 of the 51 sets with whitetips.

Twenty-five of the 50 sharks got full codes. The other 25 were told apart from the right side of the fin alone.

Twelve sharks were filmed on two or more sets in the same season. Two of them, OWT8 and OWT15, were first filmed in 2023 and came back to the cameras in 2024.

Most camera studies report MaxN, the largest number of one species seen in a single frame. The fin count and MaxN agreed on about 87 percent of sets. Most of those had no whitetip or just one.

With two whitetips on screen at once, the counts matched only 31 percent of the time. With three, it was 28 percent. Papastamatiou said fin IDs let a researcher review the footage and say “actually, that was three different sharks.”

Fields now works at the Cape Eleuthera Institute in Rock Sound, Eleuthera. “I think what FIU did for me was solidify the importance of coming back home to work. The Bahamas is a big hub for shark research, and there are little to no Bahamians involved in that work,” she told FIU News.

Her other adviser, Demian Chapman, now works at Mote Marine Laboratory in Sarasota, Florida. Fields said both men pushed her to stick with it and become a Bahamian shark expert.

The 99 sets are a first count for whitetips in The Bahamas. They say nothing yet about whether the population is rising or falling.

On some sets no fin could be read. In those cases the team used MaxN. On three sets it told sharks apart by sex, a tag or fishing gear hanging from the body. The data are not in a public archive, and the paper says Fields will share them on reasonable request.

The authors also note that The Bahamas is a shark sanctuary but whitetips swim out of it into fished waters. In the Marshall Islands, scientists found that a shark sanctuary offered little protection from illegal fishing.

Next, the team wants cameras at more points along the archipelago, to see whether the sharks stay put or travel between islands. Fields also hopes to add photos from citizen scientists to the fin catalog.

OWT8 and OWT15 first swam up to the bonito off Cat Island in the spring of 2023. A year later both were back.

The full study was published in the journal Marine Biology.

JWoww Fights Back Tears Over 'RHOSLC' Scene Involving Mary's Son: ‘Low Blow’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Jenni ‘JWoww’ Farley struggled to keep the tears back as she watched a “low blow” unfold while watching an episode of The Real Housewives of Salt Lake City.

Posting via Instagram on Thursday, November 5, the Jersey Shore star, 41, shared a clip of RHOSLC which showed an altercation between Angie Katsanevas and Mary Cosby. During the heated exchange, Angie, 52, made reference to Mary’s son Robert Cosby Jr. (Robert Jr. died in February at age 23 following a suspected overdose.)

“I’m trying so hard not to cry,” Farley said after watching the scene as she struggled to remain composed.

She added, “What I just saw had to be the worst thing I have ever seen on reality TV. Regardless of what you think of anyone … that woman lost her child.”

Farley further blasted the choice to use the death of someone’s child in an on-screen argument as “f**king bottom of the barrel, the most low blow, and I don’t give a f**k.”

“Britani [Bateman] the last episode, now Angie, this episode…” Farley fumed.

Farley also contrasted the TV moment against the drama that often transpired on Jersey Shore, insisting that she and her costars ultimately had each other’s backs despite not always seeing eye to eye.

“This is why I believe Jersey Shore, at the end of the day, didn’t rate the way that Bravo rates, and I understand it,” she said. “Drama sells, but one thing I know about my cast, regardless of how much production hated it, we protected each other, and we had our low blows, especially before children. We had our low blows [but] I have seen twice now, women attack a woman that lost her child.”

In her post’s caption, Farley doubled down on her take that using Mary’s son’s death against her was too far.

“Crucify me in the comments as you will, but I just started really getting into watching reality TV because it’s been my career and I’m always curious how other networks film… So I take shows like this with a grain of salt… but after watching this scene on #rhoslc my heart is broken for Mary… I don’t care what your opinion is… A child’s death is off the table,” Farley wrote. “This is not an attack on Andy [Cohen] or the producers, because they make damn good TV, but people really need to watch their words… Coming from someone who has learned so many tough lessons on television…”

In February, Mary, 54, confirmed the death of her son via a statement to Us.

“Our beloved son, Robert Jr., has been called home to the Lord,” Mary shared in a statement at the time. “Though our hearts ache, we take comfort in God’s promise and in knowing he is finally at peace. We are grateful for your prayers and trust in the Lord to carry us through this time of sorrow.”

Robert Jr.’s addiction battle was featured on several seasons of RHOSLC, with viewers often applauding Mary for speaking candidly about her son’s struggles.
